WISHBONE Classic Bus Cycles\nWISHBONE Classic bus cycles are described in terms of their general operation, reset\noperation, handshaking protocol and the data organization during transfers. Additional\nrequirements for bus cycles (especially those relating to the common clock) can be found in\nthe timing specifications in Chapter 5.\n3.1     General Operation\nMASTER and SLAVE interfaces are interconnected with a set of signals that permit them\nto exchange data. For descriptive purposes these signals are cumulatively known as a bus,\nand are contained within a functional module called the INTERCON. Address, data and\nother information is impressed upon this bus in the form of bus cycles.\n\n3.1.1     Reset Operation\nAll hardware interfaces are initialized to a pre-defined state. This is accomplished with the\nreset signal [RST_O] that can be asserted at any time. It is also used for test simulation\npurposes by initializing all self-starting state machines and counters which may be used in\nthe design. The reset signal [RST_O] is driven by the SYSCON module. It is connected to\nthe [RST_I] signal on all MASTER and SLAVE interfaces. Illustration 3-1: Reset cycle\nshows the reset cycle.\n\n\n                                                              NOTE(2)\n                                  CLK_I           0                1    NOTE(1)    2                3\n\n\n\n\n                 MASTER SIGNALS\n                                  RST_I\n                                                                                                  NOTE(3)\n                                  STB_O\n\n\n                                  CYC_O\n\n                                          NOTES:\n                                          (1) Reset cycles can be extended for any length of time.\n                                          (2) Self-starting state machines & counters reset themselves at\n                                              the rising [CLK_I] edge following the assertion of [RST_I].\n                                              On MASTERs,[STB_O] and [CYC_O] are negated at the same time.\n                                          (3) On MASTERs, [STB_O] and [CYC_O] may be asserted at the\n                                              rising [CLK_I] edge following the negation of [RST_I].\n\n                                              Illustration 3-1: Reset cycle\n\n   RULE 3.00\nAll WISHBONE interfaces MUST initialize themselves at the rising [CLK_I] edge following\nthe assertion of [RST_I]. They MUST stay in the initialized state until the rising [CLK_I]\nedge that follows the negation of [RST_I].\n\n   RULE 3.05\n[RST_I] MUST be asserted for at least one complete clock cycle on all WISHBONE\ninterfaces.\n\n   PERMISSION 3.00\n[RST_I] MAY be asserted for more than one clock cycle, and MAY be asserted indefinitely.\n\n\n     Back to TOC                                Copyright © 2010 OpenCores                                   Page 31 / 128\n\f"},{"pdf_page":32,"text":"                                       Wishbone B4\n\n\n   RULE 3.10\nAll WISHBONE interfaces MUST be capable of reacting to [RST_I] at any time.\n\n   RULE 3.15\nAll self-starting state machines and counters in WISHBONE interfaces MUST initialize\nthemselves at the rising [CLK_I] edge following the assertion of [RST_I]. They MUST stay\nin the initialized state until the rising [CLK_I] edge that follows the negation of [RST_I].\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.00\nIn general, self-starting state machines do not need to be initialized. However, this may\ncause problems because some simulators may not be sophisticated enough to find an initial\nstarting point for the state machine. Furthermore, self-starting state machines can go\nthrough an indeterminate number of initialization cycles before finding their starting state,\nthereby making it difficult to predict their behavior at start-up time. The initialization rule\nprevents both problems by forcing all state machines to a pre-defined state in response to\nthe assertion of [RST_I].\n\n   RULE 3.20\nThe following MASTER signals MUST be negated at the rising [CLK_I] edge following the\nassertion of [RST_I], and MUST stay in the negated state until the rising [CLK_I] edge\nthat follows the negation of [RST_I]: [STB_O], [CYC_O]. The state of all other MASTER\nsignals are undefined in response to a reset cycle.\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.05\nOn MASTER interfaces [STB_O] and [CYC_O] may be asserted beginning at the rising\n[CLK_I] edge following the negation of [RST_I].\n\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.10\nSLAVE interfaces automatically negate [ACK_O], [ERR_O] and [RTY_O] when their\n[STB_I] is negated in standard mode.\nIn pipelined mode the assertion of [ACK_O], [ERR_O] and [RTY_O] is done\nindependently of [STB_I].\n\n   RECOMENDATION 3.00\nDesign SYSCON modules so that they assert [RST_O] during a power-up condition.\n[RST_O] should remain asserted until all voltage levels and clock frequencies in the system\nare stabilized. When negating [RST_O], do so in a synchronous manner that conforms to\nthis specification.\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.15\nIf a gated clock generator is used, and if the clock is stopped, then the WISHBONE\ninterface is not capable of responding to its [RST_I] signal.\n\n   SUGGESTION 3.00\nSome circuits require an asynchronous reset capability. If an IP core or other SoC\ncomponent requires an asynchronous reset, then define it as a non-WISHBONE signal.\nThis prevents confusion with the WISHBONE reset [RST_I] signal that uses a purely\nsynchronous protocol, and needs to be applied to the WISHBONE interface only.\n\n\n     Back to TOC               Copyright © 2010 OpenCores                Page 32 / 128\n\f"},{"pdf_page":33,"text":"                                      Wishbone B4\n\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.20\nAll WISHBONE interfaces respond to the reset signal. However, the IP Core connected to\na WISHBONE interface does not necessarily need to respond to the reset signal.\n\n3.1.2     Transfer Cycle initiation\nMASTER interfaces initiate a transfer cycle by asserting [CYC_O]. When [CYC_O] is\nnegated, all other MASTER signals are invalid.\nSLAVE interfaces respond to other SLAVE signals only when [CYC_I] is asserted.\nSYSCON signals and responses to SYSCON signals are not affected.\n\n   RULE 3.25\nMASTER interfaces MUST assert [CYC_O] for the duration of SINGLE READ / WRITE,\nBLOCK and RMW cycles. [CYC_O] MUST be asserted no later than the rising [CLK_I]\nedge that qualifies the assertion of [STB_O]. [CYC_O] MUST be negated no earlier than\nthe rising [CLK_I] edge that qualifies the negation of [STB_O].\n\n   PERMISSION 3.05\nMASTER interfaces MAY assert [CYC_O] indefinitely.\n\n   RECOMMENDATION 3.05\nArbitration logic often uses [CYC_I] to select between MASTER interfaces. Keeping\n[CYC_O] asserted may lead to arbitration problems. It is therefore recommended that\n[CYC_O] is not indefinitely asserted.\n\n   RULE 3.30\nSLAVE interfaces MAY NOT respond to any SLAVE signals when [CYC_I] is negated.\nHowever, SLAVE interfaces MUST always respond to SYSCON signals.\n\n3.1.3     Handshaking Protocol\nAll bus cycles use a handshaking protocol between the MASTER and SLAVE interfaces.\nThere exists two different traffic modes, standard and pipelined.\n\n3.1.3.1   Standard wishbone protocol\n\nAs shown in Figure 3-2, the MASTER asserts [STB_O] when it is ready to transfer data.\n[STB_O] remains asserted until the SLAVE asserts one of the cycle terminating signals\n[ACK_I], [ERR_I] or [RTY_I]. At every rising edge of [CLK_I] the terminating signal is\nsampled. If it is asserted, then [STB_O] is negated. This gives both MASTER and SLAVE\ninterfaces the possibility to control the rate at which data is transferred.\n\n\n\n\n        Illustration 3-2: Standard bus handshaking protocol, asynchronous slave.\n\n\n\n     Back to TOC              Copyright © 2010 OpenCores            Page 33 / 128\n\f"},{"pdf_page":34,"text":"                                      Wishbone B4\n\n\n\n\n      Illustration 3-3: Standard wishbone handshaking protocol, synchronous slave.\n\n\n\n   PERMISSION 3.10\nIf in standard mode the SLAVE guarantees it can keep pace with all MASTER interfaces\nand if the [ERR_I] and [RTY_I] signals are not used, then the SLAVE’s [ACK_O] signal\nMAY be tied to the logical AND of the SLAVE’s [STB_I] and [CYC_I] inputs. The interface\nwill function normally under these circumstances.\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.25\nIn standard mode SLAVE interfaces assert a cycle termination signal in response to\n[STB_I]. However, [STB_I] is only valid when [CYC_I] is valid.\n\n   RULE 3.35\nIn standard mode the cycle termination signals [ACK_O], [ERR_O], and [RTY_O] must be\ngenerated in response to the logical AND of [CYC_I] and [STB_I].\n\n   PERMISSION 3.15\nOther signals, besides [CYC_I] and [STB_I], MAY be included in the generation of the\ncycle termination signals.\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.30\nInternal SLAVE signals also determine what cycle termination signal is asserted and when\nit is asserted.\nMost of the examples in this specification describe the use of [ACK_I] to terminate a local\nbus cycle. However, the SLAVE can optionally terminate the cycle with an error [ERR_O],\nor request that the cycle be retried [RTY_O].\nAll MASTER interfaces include the [ACK_I] terminator signal. Asserting this signal during\na bus cycle causes it to terminate normally.\nAsserting the [ERR_I] signal during a bus cycle will terminate the cycle. It also serves to\nnotify the MASTER that an error occurred during the cycle. This signal is generally used if\nan error was detected by SLAVE logic circuitry. For example, if the SLAVE is a parity-\nprotected memory, then the [ERR_I] signal can be asserted if a parity fault is detected.\nThis specification does not dictate what the MASTER will do in response to [ERR_I].\nAsserting the optional [RTY_I] signal during a bus cycle will terminate the cycle. It also\nserves to notify the MASTER that the current cycle should be aborted, and retried at a later\ntime. This signal is generally used for shared memory and bus bridges. In these cases\nSLAVE circuitry asserts [RTY_I] if the local resource is busy. This specification does not\ndictate when or how the MASTER will respond to [RTY_I].\n\n   RULE 3.40\nAs a minimum, the MASTER interface MUST include the following signals: [ACK_I],\n\n\n     Back to TOC               Copyright © 2010 OpenCores              Page 34 / 128\n\f"},{"pdf_page":35,"text":"                                       Wishbone B4\n\n\n[CLK_I], [CYC_O], [RST_I], and [STB_O]. As a minimum, the SLAVE interface MUST\ninclude the following signals: [ACK_O], [CLK_I], [CYC_I], [STB_I], and [RST_I]. All\nother signals are optional.\n\n   PERMISSION 3.20\nMASTER and SLAVE interfaces MAY be designed to support the [ERR_I] and [ERR_O]\nsignals. In these cases, the SLAVE asserts [ERR_O] to indicate that an error has occurred\nduring the bus cycle. This specification does not dictate what the MASTER does in\nresponse to [ERR_I].\n\n   PERMISSION 3.25\nMASTER and SLAVE interfaces MAY be designed to support the [RTY_I] and [RTY_O]\nsignals. In these cases, the SLAVE asserts [RTY_O] to indicate that the interface is busy,\nand that the bus cycle should be retried at a later time. This specification does not dictate\nwhat the MASTER will do in response to [RTY_I].\n\n   RULE 3.45\nIf a SLAVE supports the [ERR_O] or [RTY_O] signals, then the SLAVE MUST NOT assert\nmore than one of the following signals at any time: [ACK_O], [ERR_O] or [RTY_O].\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.35\nIf the SLAVE supports the [ERR_O] or [RTY_O] signals, but the MASTER does not\nsupport these signals, deadlock may occur.\n\n   RECOMMENDATION 3.10\nDesign INTERCON modules to prevent deadlock conditions. One solution to this problem\nis to include a watchdog timer function that monitors the MASTER’s [STB_O] signal, and\nasserts [ERR_I] or [RTY_I] if the cycle exceeds some pre-defined time limit. INTERCON\nmodules can also be designed to disconnect interfaces from the WISHBONE bus if they\nconstantly generate bus errors and/or watchdog time-outs.\n\n   RECOMMENDATION 3.15\nDesign WISHBONE MASTER interfaces so that there are no intermediate logic gates\nbetween a registered flip-flop and the signal outputs on [STB_O] and [CYC_O]. Delay\ntiming for [STB_O] and [CYC_O] are very often the most critical paths in the system. This\nprevents sloppy design practices from slowing down the interconnect because of added\ndelays on these two signals.\n\n   RULE 3.50\nSLAVE interfaces MUST be designed so that the [ACK_O], [ERR_O], and [RTY_O] signals\nare asserted and negated in response to the assertion and negation of [STB_I].\n\n   PERMISSION 3.30\nThe assertion of [ACK_O], [ERR_O], and [RTY_O] MAY be asynchronous to the [CLK_I]\nsignal (i.e. there is a combinatorial logic path between [STB_I] and [ACK_O]).\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.40\nThe asynchronous assertion of [ACK_O], [ERR_O], and [RTY_O] assures that the\ninterface can accomplish one data transfer per clock cycle. Furthermore, it simplifies the\ndesign of arbiters in multi-MASTER applications.\n\n     Back to TOC               Copyright © 2010 OpenCores                Page 35 / 128\n\f"},{"pdf_page":36,"text":"                                        Wishbone B4\n\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.45\nThe asynchronous assertion of [ACK_O], [ERR_O], and [RTY_O] could proof impossible\nto implement. For example slave wait states are easiest implemented using a registered\n[ACK_O] signal.\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.50\nIn large high speed designs the asynchronous assertion of [ACK_O], [ERR_O], and\n[RTY_O] could lead to unacceptable delay times, caused by the loopback delay from the\nMASTER to the SLAVE and back to the MASTER. Using registered [ACK_O], [ERR_O],\nand [RTY_O] signals significantly reduces this loopback delay, at the cost of one additional\nwait state per transfer. See WISHBONE Registered Feedback Bus Cycles for methods of\neliminating the wait state.\n\n   PERMISSION 3.35\nUnder certain circumstances SLAVE interfaces MAY be designed to hold [ACK_O] in the\nasserted state. This situation occurs on point-to-point interfaces where there is a single\nSLAVE on the interface, and that SLAVE always operates without wait states.\n\n   RULE 3.55\nMASTER interfaces MUST be designed to operate normally when the SLAVE interface\nholds [ACK_I] in the asserted state.\n\n3.1.3.2     Pipelined wishbone protocol\n\nIn pipelined mode the handshake protocol is somewhat different.\n   ●    The master doesn't wait for [ACK_I] before outputting next address/data word on\n        the bus\n   ●    we have a [STALL_I] input in the master, indicating that the slave pipeline can\n        accept another request at the moment. For a bus arbiter a multiplexer should be\n        used to select the targeted slave [STALL_O] signal and feed that to the master. This\n        path should be non registered.\n   ●    master outputs the requests as long as [STALL_I] is low. When [STALL_I] is\n        asserted, it waits. Also, the pipeline in the interconnect doesn't push any requests\n        when [STALL_I] is asserted.\n   ●    cycle consisting of N transactions is terminated with a sequence of N [ACK_I].\n        Subsequent [ACK_I] indicate the finished transactions. Master terminates the bus\n        cycle (by deasserting [CYC_O]) after receiving the last [ACK_I] pulse.\n   ●    read data is valid when [ACK_I] is high.\n\n\n\n\n       Back to TOC              Copyright © 2010 OpenCores               Page 36 / 128\n\f"},{"pdf_page":37,"text":"                                       Wishbone B4\n\n\n\n\n                       Illustration 3-4: Pipelined mode, single access\n\n\n\n   RULE 3.57\nIn pipelined mode a read transaction is started when the following signals are asserted\n       [CYC_I], [STB_I]\nand the following negated\n       [STALL_I], [WE_I].\n\n   RULE 3.58\nIn pipelined mode a write transaction is started when the following signals are asserted\n       [CYC_I], [STB_I], [WE_I]\nand the following negated\n       [STALL_I].\n\n   RULE 3.59\nIn pipelined mode the MASTER must accept [ACK_I] signals at any time after a\ntransaction is initiated.\n\n3.1.4     Use of [STB_O]\n\n   RULE 3.60\nMASTER interfaces MUST qualify the following signals with [STB_O]: [ADR_O],\n[DAT_O()], [SEL_O()], [WE_O], and [TAGN_O].\n\n   PERMISSION 3.40\nIf a MASTER doesn’t generate wait states, then [STB_O] and [CYC_O] MAY be assigned\nthe same signal.\n\n   OBSERVATION 3.55\n[CYC_O] needs to be asserted during the entire transfer cycle.      return ('K12: master %d ACK retires slave_canceled entry accepted@%s' % (m, e['row']), 'K12_batch_end')\n    if st in ('unf', 'fwd'):\n        return ('K7: master %d ACK retires entry accepted@%s from bound slave ACK data %r (entry accepted@%s)' % (m, md, e['sd'], e['row']), 'K7_ack_basis_read_data')\n    h['mq'][m].pop(0)\n    e['loc'] = 'ret'\n    return None\n\n\ndef _err(h, eid, m, c):\n    e = h['E'][eid]\n    h['mq'][m].pop(0)\n    e['loc'] = 'ret'\n    if e['map'] is None or e['st'] == 'serr':\n        return\n    h['tags'].append(['unexplained_mapped_err', c, 'master %d ERR retired mapped entry accepted@%s in state %s with no slave ERR; timeout/starvation conditions and precedence unknown' % (m, e['row'], e['st'])])\n\n\ndef check_rows(rows, limits=None):\n    failures = []\n    av = []\n    unresolved = []\n    events = []\n    cnt = collections.Counter()\n    lim = dict(DEFAULT_LIMITS)\n    bad_lim = None\n    if limits is not None:\n        if not isinstance(limits, dict):\n            bad_lim = 'limits must be a dictionary'\n        else:\n            for k in limits:\n                v = limits[k]\n                if k not in DEFAULT_LIMITS:\n                    bad_lim = 'unknown limit name %r' % (k,)\n                elif isinstance(v, bool) or not isinstance(v, int) or v < 1:\n                    bad_lim = 'limit %s must be a positive integer' % k\n                else:\n                    lim[k] = v\n    S = {'H': None, 'mode': 'pre_reset', 'rep': 0, 'mep': [0, 0], 'nid': 0,\n         'idm': collections.Counter(), 'ids': collections.Counter(), 'sfq': [[], []],\n         'cacc': [[], []], 'cack': [0, 0], 'cnon': [0, 0], 'chr': [0, 0],\n         'susp': 0, 'dead': 0, 'exh': [], 'cl_unk': set(), 'cl_susp': set(),\n         'e': None, 'prefix': None, list(clauses)})\n\n    def env(c, cat, detail):\n        av.append({'cycle': c, 'category': cat, 'detail': detail})\n\n    def unr(c, item, detail, clause):\n        unresolved.append({'cycle': c, 'item': item, 'detail': detail})\n        if clause:\n            S['cl_unk'].add(clause)\n\n    def sub(name):\n        d = S['sub'].setdefault(name, [0, 0, 0, 0])\n        d[0] += 1\n        if not SUBS[name][1] or S['mode'] == 'active':\n            d[1] += 1\n        elif S['mode'] == 'suspended':\n            d[2] += 1\n        else:\n            d[3] += 1\n\n    def sizes_of(H):\n        live = 0\n        pool = 0\n        for h in H:\n            for q in h['mq']:\n                if len(q) > live:\n                    live = len(q)\n            if len(h['pool']) > pool:\n                pool = len(h['pool'])\n        return {'hypotheses': len(H), 'max_live_per_master': live, 'max_canceled_pool': pool}\n\n    def exhaust(c, sz, point, projected=None):\n        H0 = S['H'] or []\n        agg0 = _agg(H0)\n        n0 = len(H0)\n        sz = dict(sz)\n        pj = projected if projected is not None else n0\n        sz['actual_hypotheses'] = n0\n        sz['projected_hypotheses'] = pj\n        for key in agg0:\n            unr(key[1], key[0], '%s [class unknown; carried by %d of %d actual histories in the %s snapshot at cycle %s (projected hypothesis count %d); reset_epoch %d; retained pre-suspension evidence]' % (key[2], agg0[key], n0, point, c, pj, S['rep']), TAG_CLAUSE.get(key[0], 'unknown_tag'))\n        alts.append({'reset_epoch': S['rep'], 'closed_at': c, 'reason': 'suspended_on_limit', 'snapshot': point, 'actual_hypotheses': n0, 'projected_hypotheses': pj, 'surviving_histories': n0, 'ownership': 'not_reported_suspended', 'histories_total': n0, 'histories_displayed': 0, 'histories_omitted': n0, 'tag_kinds_before_suspension': sorted(set(k[0] for k in agg0))})\n        S['mode'] = 'suspended'\n        S['H'] = None\n        S['rowtags'] = None\n        S['exh'].append({'cycle': c, 'reset_epoch': S['rep'], 'sizes': sz, 'snapshot': point, 'actual_hypotheses': n0, 'projected_hypotheses': pj})\n        S['cl_susp'].update(ASSOC)\n        unresolved.append({'cycle': c, 'item': 'unresolved_on_limit', 'detail': 'resource limit exceeded at cycle %s (%s snapshot; actual hypotheses %d, projected %d), sizes=%r, limits=%r; no pruning or branch selection; K4 per-candidate consumption, K6, K7, K8 attribution, K9 tags and K12 suspended for all masters until reset; earlier violations stay final' % (c, point, n0, pj, sz, lim)})\n\n    def exhaust_check(c):\n        if S['mode'] != 'active':\n            return\n        sz = sizes_of(S['H'])\n        if sz['hypotheses'] > lim['MAX_HYP'] or sz['max_live_per_master'] > lim['MAX_LIVE_PER_MASTER'] or sz['max_canceled_pool'] > lim['MAX_CANCELED_POOL']:\n            exhaust(c, sz, 'post_expansion_step_check')\n\n    def death_check(c, wits):\n        if S['mode'] == 'active' and not S['H']:\n            S['mode'] = 'dead'\n            cls = sorted(set(w[1] for w in wits))\n            txt = '; '.join(w[0] for w in wits[:WIT]) + ' [witnesses shown %d of %d, omitted %d]' % (min(len(wits), WIT), len(wits), max(0, len(wits) - WIT))\n            rt = S['rowtags']\n            rn = 0\n            if rt:\n                ragg, rn = rt\n                for key in ragg:\n                    S['disc'].append({'reset_epoch': S['rep'], 'tag': key[0], 'tag_row': key[1], 'detail': key[2], 'carried_by': ragg[key], 'of_histories': rn, 'status': 'all carrying histories refuted at cycle %s by concrete pin contradiction; disclosure only, not an open unknown and not a hidden-owner fact' % c, 'death_cycle': c})\n            alts.append({'reset_epoch': S['rep'], 'closed_at': c, 'reason': 'membership_dead', 'surviving_histories': 0, 'histories_alive_at_row_start': rn, 'dying_row_tags': 'not_captured: tags created in row %s by branches that all died in row %s are omitted' % (c, c), 'ownership': 'none_surviving', 'histories_total': 0, 'histories_displayed': 0, 'histories_omitted': 0})\n            fail(c, 'membership_empty_H', 'no surviving explicit history at cycle %s; per-branch witnesses: %s; clauses used: %s (K4 per-candidate, K6, K7, K8 and K12 are inferred)' % (c, txt, ', '.join(cls)), False, cls)\n\n    def close_epoch(c, end):\n        if S['mode'] != 'active' or not S['H']:\n            return\n        H = S['H']\n        n = len(H)\n        S['rowtags'] = None\n        agg = collections.OrderedDict()\n        for h in H:\n            seen = set()\n            for t in h['tags']:\n                key = (t[0], t[1], t[2])\n                if key in seen:\n                    continue\n                seen.add(key)\n                agg[key] = agg.get(key, 0) + 1\n        for key in agg:\n            unr(key[1], key[0], '%s [class unknown; carried by %d of %d surviving histories; reset_epoch %d]' % (key[2], agg[key], n, S['rep']), TAG_CLAUSE.get(key[0], 'unknown_tag'))\n        pend = []\n        scan = 0\n        for h in H:\n            p = 0\n            for q in h['mq']:\n                for eid in q:\n                    if h['E'][eid]['st'] == 'scan':\n                        scan += 1\n                    else:\n                        p += 1\n            pend.append(p)\n        alts.append({'reset_epoch': S['rep'], 'closed_at': c, 'reason': 'batch_end' if end else 'reset', 'surviving_histories': n, 'ownership': 'unique_by_elimination' if n == 1 else 'indistinguishable(%d)' % n, 'pending_live_per_history': pend[:DISP], 'tag_sets': [sorted(set(t[0] for t in h['tags'])) for h in H[:DISP]], 'histories_total': n, 'histories_displayed': min(n, DISP), 'histories_omitted': max(0, n - DISP)})\n        if end:\n            cnt['act_K12_batch_end'] += 1\n            sub('K12_batch_end')\n            if max(pend) > 0:\n                unr(c, 'pending_at_batch_end', 'live entries not retired at trace end in surviving histories (min %d, max %d); inconclusive, never a liveness failure' % (min(pend), max(pend)), 'K12_batch_end')\n            cnt['slave_canceled_live_at_end'] += scan\n        else:\n            cnt['live_discarded_by_reset'] += sum(pend) + scan\n\n    def clean():\n        if failures or unresolved or S['mode'] != 'active':\n            return False\n        for h in S['H']:\n            if h['tags']:\n                return False\n            for q in h['mq']:\n                for eid in q:\n                    if h['E'][eid]['st'] != 'scan':\n                        return False\n        return True\n\n    def new_epoch():\n        S['H'] = [_newh()]\n        S['mode'] = 'active'\n        S['idm'] = collections.Counter()\n        S['ids'] = collections.Counter()\n        S['sfq'] = [[], []]\n        S['cacc'] = [[], []]\n        S['cack'] = [0, 0]\n        S['cnon'] = [0, 0]\n        S['chr'] = [0, 0]\n\n    def boundary(c, nf, snap):\n        S['e'] = c\n        keep = failures[:nf]\n        mixed = []\n        for f in failures[nf:]:\n            if f['pin']:\n                keep.append(f)\n            else:\n                mixed.append(f)\n        failures[:] = keep\n        for f in mixed:\n            unresolved.append({'cycle': c, 'item': 'mixed_same_edge', 'detail': 'mixed_same_edge(dut_candidate, env): %s: %s; no for f in mixed)\n        if mdeath:\n            S['disc'] = [d for d in S['disc'] if d.get('death_cycle') != c]\n        rt = S['rowtags']\n        S['rowtags'] = None\n        if rt:\n            ragg, rn = rt\n            for key in ragg:\n                unr(key[1], key[0], '%s [class unknown; valid-prefix tag carried by %d of %d histories feasible at the start of boundary row %s (snapshot 0:\n            S['prefix'] = 'prefix_dut_violation'\n        elif snap:\n            S['prefix'] = 'prefix_scoped_pass'\n        else:\n            S['prefix'] = 'prefix_unresolved'\n\n    truncated = False\n    lastc = None\n    pp = None\n    pp_reset = False\n    seen_reset = False\n    nrows = len(rows) if isinstance(rows, list) else 0\n    if bad_lim:\n        env(None, 'invalid_limits', bad_lim)\n    elif not isinstance(rows, list) or not rows:\n        env(None, 'K11_env_input_validity', 'empty or non-list trace')\n    else:\n        for idx in range(len(rows)):\n            r =           c, pe, pd = _validate(r, lastc)\n            if pe or pd:\n                for d in pd:\n                    fail(c, 'K10_dut_master_handshake', 'undefined DUT control output ' + d, True, ['K10_dut_master_handshake'])\n                for d in pe:\n                    env(c, 'K11_env_input_validity', d)\n                if not pe:\n                    truncated = True\n                    unresolved.append({'cycle': c, 'item': 'unjudgeable_row', 'detail': 'undefined DUT control output; this and later rows are not judged'})\n                    break\n                boundary(c, nf, snap)\n                break\n            lastc = c\n            cnt['total_rows'] += 1\n            pre = r['pre']\n            post = r['post']\n\n            def g(k, i):\n                return _lane(pre[k], k, i)\n\n            def gp(k, i):\n                return _lane(pp[k], k, i)\n\n            reset = pre['i_reset'] == 1\n            resp_done = [False, False]\n            for m in (0, 1):\n                ra = g('o_mack', m)\n                re_ = g('o_merr', m)\n                if ra and re_:\n                    resp_done[m] = True\n                    cnt['act_K10_dut_master_handshake'] += 1\n                    sub('K10_rule_3_45')\n                    fail(c, 'K10_dut_master_handshake', 'rule 3.45: o_mack and o_merr both high on master %d; response not attributed' % m, True, ['K10_dut_master_handshake'])\n                elif (ra or re_) and not g('i_mcyc', m):\n                    resp_done[m] = True\n                    cnt['act_K13_response_without_cyc'] += 1\n                    sub('K13_response_without_cyc')\n                    if pp is None:\n                        cnt['k13_unjudged_first_row'] += 1\n                    elif not gp('i_mcyc', m):\n                        fail(c, 'K13_response_without_cyc', 'master %d %s while pre i_mcyc is low in this row and the previous row (inferred K13)' % (m, 'ACK' if ra else 'ERR'), True, ['K13_response_without_cyc'])\n                    else:\n                        unr(c, 'same_edge_response_after_abort', 'master %d %s in the row where i_mcyc fell; never retired into work' % (m, 'ACK' if ra else 'ERR'), 'K13_response_without_cyc')\n            if reset:\n                cnt['reset_rows'] += 1\n                cnt['act_K1_reset'] += 1\n                sub('K1_reset_outputs')\n                for k in ('o_scyc', 'o_sstb'):\n                    v = post[k]\n                    if v is None or v != 0:\n                        fail(c, 'K1_reset', 'post %s=%r in a reset row' % (k, v), True, ['K1_reset'])\n                if pp is not None and pp_reset and (pre['i_mcyc'] or pre['i_mstb']):\n                    env(c, 'K1_reset', 'master CYC/STB high            new_epoch()\n            elif not seen_reset:\n                env(c, 'K11_env_input_validity', 'trace does not begin with reset')\n            else:\n                cnt['nonreset_rows'] += 1\n                cnt['act_K11_env'] += 1\n                sub('K11_env_checks')\n                act = S['mode'] == 'active'\n                mcyc = [g('i_mcyc', m) for m in (0, 1)]\n                mstb = [g('i_mstb', m) for m in (0, 1)]\n                mst = [g('o_mstall', m) for m in (0, 1)]\n                mproj = [_proj(g('i_mwe', m), g('i_maddr', m), g('i_msel', m), g('i_mdata', m)) for m in (0, 1)]\n                held = [bool(mcyc[m] and mstb[m] and mst[m]) for m in (0, 1)]\n                acc = [bool(mcyc[m] and mstb[m] and not mst[m]) for m in (0, 1)]\n                pheld = [False, False]\n                pproj = [None, None]\n                if pp is not None and not pp_reset:\n                    for m in (0, 1):\n                        pheld[m] = bool(gp('i_mcyc',           hr2 = [False, False]\n                for m in (0, 1):\n                    if held[m] or acc[m]:\n                        cnt['act_K2_accept_identity'] += 1\n                        sub('K2_master_identity_env')\n                    if (held[m] or acc[m]) and mproj[m] is None:\n                        env(c, 'K2_accept_identity', 'master %d strobed request with undefined identity None and mproj[m] == pproj[m])\n                    hr[m] = held[m] or hr2[m]\n                # step 1: CYC drop\n                for m in (0, 1):\n                    if not mcyc[m]:\n                        if pp is not None and gp('i_mcyc', m):\n                            S['mep'][m] += 1\n                            cnt['act_K9_cancellation_orphans'] += 1\n                            sub('K9_cancellation')\n                        S['cacc'][m] = []\n                        S['cack'][m] = 0\n                        S['cnon'][m] = 0\n                        S['chr'][m] = 0\n                        if act:\n                            for h in S['H']:\n                                for eid in h['mq'][m]:\n                                    e = h['E'][eid]\n                                    if e['st'] == 'unf':\n                                        e['loc'] = 'pool'\n                                        e['crow'] = c\n                                        h['pool'].append(eid)\n                                    elif e['st'] == 'fwd':\n                                        e['loc'] = 'orph'\n                                    else:\n                                        e['loc'] = 'gone'\n                                h['mq'][m] = []\n                exhaust_check(c)\n                act = S['mode'] == 'active'\n                # step 2: master acceptances\n                new_eid = [None, None]\n                for m in (0, 1):\n                    if acc[m] and mproj[m] is not None:\n                        S['idm'][mproj[m]] += 1\n                        S['cacc'][m].append(c)\n                        cnt['master_accepted'] += 1\n                        eid = S['nid']\n                        S['nid'] += 1\n                        new_eid[m] = eid\n                        if act:\n                            for h in S['H']:\n                                h['E'][eid] = {'id': eid, 'm': m, 'idt': mproj[m], 'row': c, 'mep': exhaust_check(c)\n                act = S['mode'] == 'active'\n                # step 3: slave CYC withdrawal\n                scyc = [g('o_scyc', s) for s in (0, 1)]\n                for s in (0, 1):\n                    if not scyc[s]:\n                        if g('i_sack', s) or g('i_serr', s):\n                            env(c, 'K11_env', 'slave %d response while pre o_scyc is low' % s)\n                        S['sfq'][s] = []\n                        if act:\n                            for h in S['H']:\n                                for eid in h['sq'][s]:\n                                    if eid is None:\n                                        continue\n                                    e = h['E'][eid]\n                                    if e['loc'] == 'live' and e['st'] == 'fwd':\n                                        e['st'] = 'scan'\n                                h['sq'][s] = []\n                # step 4: slave responses\n                for s in (0, 1):\n                    sa = g('i_sack', s)\n                    se = g('i_serr', s)\n                    if not scyc[s] or not (sa or se):\n                        continue\n                    if sa and se:\n                        env(c, 'K11_env', 'slave %d asserted ACK and ERR together' % s)\n                        continue\n                    if not S['sfq'][s]:\n                        env(c,      if sa and head[1] == 0 and g('i_sdata', s) is None:\n                        env(c, 'K2_accept_identity', 'slave %d read ACK with undefined data; public per-slave FIFO head is the read forward if eid is None:\n                                continue\n                            e = h['E'][eid]\n                            if e['loc'] == 'live' and e['st'] == 'fwd':\n                                e['sr'] = c\n                                if sa:\n                                    e['st'] = 'sack'\n                                    e['sd'] = g('i_sdata', s)\n                                else:\n                                    e['st'] = 'serr'\n                # step 5: slave acceptances\n                for s in (0, 1):\n                    if not (scyc[s] and g('o_sstb', cnt['slave_accepted'] += 1\n                    cnt['act_K2_accept_identity'] += 1\n                    cnt['act_K3_routing'] += 1\n                    cnt['act_K4_no_invention_multiplicity'] += 1\n                    sub('K2_slave_identity')\n                    sub('K3_routing')\n                    S['sfq'][s].append((c, g('o_swe', s)))\n                    if sp is None:\n                        fail(c, 'K2_accept_identity', 'slave %d accepted request with undefined identity projection' % s, True, ['K2_accept_identity'])\n                        if act:\n                            for h in sub('K4_candidate_consumption')\n                    if S['ids'][sp] > S['idm'][sp]:\n                        fail(c, 'K4_count_projection', 'identity %s forwarded %d times but accepted %d times by plans = []\n                    total = 0\n                    for h in S['H']:\n                        cl = []\n                        for m in (0, 1):\n                            for eid in           for eid in h['pool']:\n                            if h['E'][eid]['idt'] == sp:\n                                cl.append(eid)\n                        plans.append(cl)\n                        total += len(cl)\n                    if total > lim['MAX_HYP']:\n                        sz = sizes_of(S['H'])\n                        exhaust(c, sz, 'pre_expansion_branching_precheck', total)\n                        act = False\n                        continue\n                    newH = []\n                    wits = []\n                    for j in range(len(S['H'])):\n                        h = S['H'][j]\n                        cl = plans[j]\n                        if not cl:\n                            wits.append(('K4: slave %d acceptance %s has no identity-equal live        h2 = h if k == len(cl) - 1 else copy.deepcopy(h)\n                            eid = cl[k]\n                            e = h2['E'][eid]\n                            if e['loc'] == 'pool':\n                                h2['pool'].remove(eid)\n                                e['loc'] = 'pfwd'\n                                h2['tags'].append(['post_abort_forward', c, 'slave %d consumed                               h2['tags'].append(['starvation_attributed', c, 'forward of a starvation-attributed member accepted@%s' % e['row']])\n                            else:\n
